### JurisCTC: Enhancing Legal Judgment Prediction via Cross-Domain Transfer and Contrastive Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17264v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaolu Kang, Hongtian Cai, Xiangyang Ji, Jinzhe Li, Nanfei Gu
- **Abstract**: In recent years, Unsupervised Domain Adaptation (UDA) has gained significant attention in the field of Natural Language Processing (NLP) owing to its ability to enhance model generalization across diverse domains. However, its application for knowledge transfer between distinct legal domains remains largely unexplored. To address the challenges posed by lengthy and complex legal texts and the limited availability of large-scale annotated datasets, we propose JurisCTC, a novel model designed to improve the accuracy of Legal Judgment Prediction (LJP) tasks. Unlike existing approaches, JurisCTC facilitates effective knowledge transfer across various legal domains and employs contrastive learning to distinguish samples from different domains. Specifically, for the LJP task, we enable knowledge transfer between civil and criminal law domains. Compared to other models and specific large language models (LLMs), JurisCTC demonstrates notable advancements, achieving peak accuracies of 76.59% and 78.83%, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the JurisCTC paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces JurisCTC, a novel model for Legal Judgment Prediction (LJP) that leverages cross-domain transfer learning and contrastive learning to improve accuracy. The key idea is to transfer knowledge from civil law judgments (where more data is available) to criminal law judgments (where data is scarce). JurisCTC uses a BERT-based feature extractor, a class classifier, and a domain classifier, incorporating unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA) and contrastive learning to learn domain-invariant features and discriminate between samples from different domains. Experimental results show JurisCTC outperforms existing models, including large language models (LLMs) and domain-specific baselines, in both civil and criminal law LJP tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of applying UDA with contrastive learning for cross-domain knowledge transfer between different legal domains (civil and criminal law) within the LJP task has a reasonable degree of novelty. While UDA and contrastive learning are established techniques in NLP, their specific application to this legal domain, combined with the architectural choices of JurisCTC, constitutes a non-trivial contribution. The integration of these techniques to address the data scarcity problem in criminal law LJP is a key differentiating factor.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem: the lack of criminal law judgment data limiting the development of accurate LJP models. The results demonstrate that JurisCTC can effectively transfer knowledge from civil law, improving the accuracy of criminal law LJP. This has practical implications for legal professionals and the public, potentially reducing legal costs and improving access to justice. The reported improvements in accuracy are substantial and suggest that the model could be a valuable tool. The paper also explores the interoperability of different legal fields through cross-domain learning, opening new avenues for interdisciplinary research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of data scarcity in criminal law LJP and the potential benefits of transfer learning.
    *   **Methodology:** The JurisCTC model is well-described, and the integration of UDA and contrastive learning is justified.
    *   **Experimental Evaluation:** The experiments are comprehensive, with comparisons against strong baselines, including recent LLMs and domain-specific models. The use of multiple evaluation metrics provides a robust assessment of performance. The ablation study is also useful for understanding the contribution of different components of the model.
    *   **Results:** The reported improvements in accuracy, recall, and F1-score over baselines are significant and convincing.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Comparison:** While the comparison against LLMs (GPT-4o, Gemini, DeepSeek-V3-Chat) is valuable, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the failure cases and the specific reasoning abilities (or lack thereof) demonstrated by LLMs versus JurisCTC. Why do LLMs sometimes perform worse despite their vast pre-training data? A qualitative analysis could strengthen the paper.
    *   **Ablation Study Depth:** While the ablation study provides some insights, more detailed analysis of the impact of each component (particularly the contrastive loss and adversarial training) on specific data subsets and tasks could give further insights into the model's strengths and weaknesses. The differences between BERT-UDA and BERT-CL are not thoroughly investigated.
    *   **Dataset Composition Details:** While the statistical overview of the dataset is given, a more detailed characterization of the specific *types* of cases included in each dataset (both Civil and Criminal) could add helpful context. For example, were the civil cases mostly contract disputes or personal injury claims? This would help with understanding the applicability of the approach.
    *   **Generalization to other legal domains:** The paper focusses on Criminal to Civil law transfer, it would be insightful to see how JurisCTC can be applied to other areas of law, such as Tax or IP law.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity Analysis:** The specific choices of hyperparameters (e.g., the temperature parameter in the contrastive loss) are not discussed in detail. A sensitivity analysis would help demonstrate the robustness of the results.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LJP and cross-domain NLP for legal applications. The JurisCTC model provides a promising approach for addressing data scarcity and improving accuracy, and it opens up new avenues for exploring the interoperability of different legal domains.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LJP by demonstrating the effectiveness of cross-domain transfer learning and contrastive learning. The JurisCTC model achieves substantial improvements in accuracy over strong baselines, including recent LLMs. It addresses a practical problem of data scarcity in criminal law LJP and has the potential to be a valuable tool for legal professionals. However, the paper could be strengthened by a more in-depth qualitative analysis of LLM performance, a more detailed ablation study, and a more nuanced description of the datasets used. This would give better understanding of the model's strengths and weaknesses and make the results more convincing. Also, demonstrating the applicability of JurisCTC to other areas of law will boost impact factor of the paper.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Generalized and Training-Free Text-Guided Semantic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17269v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Hong, Xiao Cai, Pengpeng Zeng, Shuai Zhang, Jingkuan Song, Lianli Gao, Heng Tao Shen
- **Abstract**: Text-guided semantic manipulation refers to semantically editing an image generated from a source prompt to match a target prompt, enabling the desired semantic changes (e.g., addition, removal, and style transfer) while preserving irrelevant contents. With the powerful generative capabilities of the diffusion model, the task has shown the potential to generate high-fidelity visual content. Nevertheless, existing methods either typically require time-consuming fine-tuning (inefficient), fail to accomplish multiple semantic manipulations (poorly extensible), and/or lack support for different modality tasks (limited generalizability). Upon further investigation, we find that the geometric properties of noises in the diffusion model are strongly correlated with the semantic changes. Motivated by this, we propose a novel $\textit{GTF}$ for text-guided semantic manipulation, which has the following attractive capabilities: 1) $\textbf{Generalized}$: our $\textit{GTF}$ supports multiple semantic manipulations (e.g., addition, removal, and style transfer) and can be seamlessly integrated into all diffusion-based methods (i.e., Plug-and-play) across different modalities (i.e., modality-agnostic); and 2) $\textbf{Training-free}$: $\textit{GTF}$ produces high-fidelity results via simply controlling the geometric relationship between noises without tuning or optimization. Our extensive experiments demonstrate the efficacy of our approach, highlighting its potential to advance the state-of-the-art in semantics manipulation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel "Generalized and Training-Free" (GTF) framework for text-guided semantic manipulation across various modalities (image, video, 3D). GTF aims to address limitations of existing methods that often require time-consuming fine-tuning, struggle with multiple semantic manipulations, and/or lack generalizability across modalities.  The core idea is to leverage the observation that the geometric properties of noise in diffusion models are strongly correlated with semantic changes. GTF works by explicitly controlling the geometric relationship between noises predicted under source and target prompts, enabling semantic addition and removal in a "plug-and-play" manner without any training. The authors categorize semantic editing into addition and removal and design noise composition strategies for each. Experiments demonstrate GTF's effectiveness in generating high-fidelity visual content while performing complex semantic edits across different modalities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of directly manipulating noise vectors based on the geometric relationship between source and target prompts is a valuable contribution. Several existing methods manipulate noise, but the precise *geometric interpretation* and the explicit focus on *semantic addition and removal as noise operations* seems more principled. The key novelty lies in the decomposition of noise vectors, which allows the proposed GTF to steer the diffusion process toward a target semantic direction. The training-free aspect is also valuable, as it makes GTF more accessible and adaptable.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from GTF's improved generalizability and simplicity. The ability to perform complex semantic edits in a training-free manner, across diverse modalities, addresses major limitations in the field. This opens opportunities for broader application of diffusion-based editing in real-world scenarios. The approach could simplify the development pipeline for creative applications and improve user experience. While many existing methods have addressed individual challenges, the claimed unified framework has the potential to be impactful. However, claims of superiority across *all* modalities require strong empirical evidence.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Generalizability:** The unified framework for different modalities (image, video, 3D) is a significant strength.
    *   **Training-Free:** Avoiding fine-tuning is a major advantage, reducing computational cost and improving accessibility.
    *   **High Fidelity Results:** The reported visual quality and quantitative metrics suggest good performance.
    *   **Theoretical Grounding:** The link to score-based generative modeling and the interpretation of noise vectors as semantic directions offers a solid theoretical justification.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Diffusion Models:** The method is inherently tied to diffusion models, so improvements or limitations in diffusion technology directly impact GTF.
    *   **Parameter Tuning:** While training-free, it relies on two weights (w1, w2), which need to be carefully tuned to balance source and target semantics. Ablation study shows that performance varies considerably based on w1 and w2. While authors provide some guidelines, the user experience aspect needs more attention to remove potential usability barriers.
    *   **Limited Scope for Unforeseen Scenarios:** While GTF demonstrated the capability in text-driven manipulations, there are unexplored directions that go beyond simple operations. Examples include precise control of semantic location, multiple prompts, style manipulations of a region that is described in the prompt or more challenging removal scenarios (i.e. removing shadows).
    *   **Quantitative Metric Issues:** The CLIP-based metrics, while standard, don't perfectly capture nuanced semantic alignment and might be gamed. While user studies provide insights, they rely on subjective opinions.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially impactful approach. GTF addresses key limitations in text-guided semantic manipulation with its generalizability and training-free operation. While some practical limitations and potential dependency on parameter tuning exist, the core ideas offer a strong framework for future research. The impact is not yet fully realized. The strength of theoretical grounding provides high confidence for the method and its robustness. Therefore, the score reflects a meaningful contribution to the field but acknowledges areas for improvement.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Combining Static and Dynamic Approaches for Mining and Testing Constraints for RESTful API Testing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17287v1)
- **Authors**: Hieu Huynh, Tri Le, Vu Nguyen, Tien N. Nguyen
- **Abstract**: In API testing, deriving logical constraints on API response bodies is crucial in generating the test cases to cover various aspects of RESTful APIs. However, existing approaches are limited to dynamic analysis in which constraints are extracted from the execution of APIs as part of the system under test. The key limitation of such a dynamic approach is its under-estimation in which inputs in API executions are not sufficiently diverse to uncover actual constraints on API response bodies. In this paper, we propose to combine a novel static analysis approach (in which the constraints for API response bodies are mined from API specifications), with the dynamic approach (which relies on API execution data). We leverage large language models (LLMs) to comprehend the API specifications, mine constraints for response bodies, and generate test cases. To reduce LLMs' hallucination, we apply an Observation-Confirmation (OC) scheme which uses initial prompts to contextualize constraints. %, allowing subsequent prompts to more accurately confirm their presence. Our empirical results show that~LLMs with OC prompting achieve high precision in constraint mining with the average of 91.2%. When combining static and dynamic analysis, our tool, RBCTest , achieves a precision of 78.5%. RBCTest detects 107 constraints that the dynamic approach misses and 46 more precise constraints. We also use its generated test cases to detect 21 mismatches between the API specification and actual response data for 8 real-world APIs. Four of the mismatches were, in fact, reported in developers' forums.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RBCTEST, a novel approach that combines static and dynamic analysis to mine constraints for RESTful API testing.  It addresses the limitations of purely dynamic approaches (like AGORA) which can under-estimate constraints due to limited diversity in execution data.  RBCTEST leverages large language models (LLMs) to extract constraints from API specifications (OpenAPI Specification - OAS) and then integrates these with dynamic analysis from AGORA.  The paper employs an Observation-Confirmation (OC) scheme to improve the precision of LLM-based constraint mining and includes mechanisms to filter invalid LLM suggestions and semantically verify LLM generated test cases with OAS examples to reduce LLM hallucinations. The approach generates test cases to validate mined constraints and reports detected mismatches between API specifications and actual API behavior. The tool is evaluated on a set of real-world APIs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the combination of static (LLM-based) and dynamic (execution-based) analysis for constraint mining in APIs. While static analysis, dynamic analysis, and LLMs have been individually applied to API testing, the integrated approach is a significant step forward.  The application of LLMs with the OC scheme and semantic verification to improve the accuracy of constraint mining is also novel. The approach provides a good balance between leveraging the broad understanding of specifications by LLMs with the specifics of runtime behavior.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in API testing: ensuring the logical correctness of API responses beyond simple status code and schema validation.  The ability to automatically derive constraints and generate tests improves the thoroughness of API testing and can identify discrepancies between specification and implementation. Finding those discrepancies (and even reporting some already described in forums) show a clear practical impact. The benchmark dataset made public enhances reproducibility and allows future comparison.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Combined Approach:**  The fusion of static and dynamic analysis effectively addresses the limitations of each individual approach.
    * **LLM Enhancement:** The OC scheme and semantic verifier significantly improve the precision of LLM-based constraint mining.
    * **Empirical Evaluation:**  Extensive evaluation on real-world APIs demonstrates the effectiveness of the approach. The authors provide a strong comparison against a baseline (AGORA) and analyze the specific contributions of each component.
    * **Practical Relevance:** The identification of real-world mismatches highlights the practical value of RBCTEST.
    * **Artifacts and Data:**  Making data and code available promotes reproducibility and future research.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on Specification Quality:** The static analysis component relies heavily on the quality and completeness of the API specification.  If the specification is incomplete or inaccurate, the static analysis will be limited. While the inclusion of dynamic analysis mitigates this to some extent, the overall effectiveness is still specification-dependent.
    * **LLM Limitations:** LLMs are not perfect. They can still hallucinate or misinterpret information, despite the OC scheme. While the semantic verifier helps, it may not catch all errors. The reliance on a specific LLM model (GPT-4-turbo) can cause reproducibility concerns for others who have different access or different model versions in the future.
    * **Scalability:** The computational cost of using LLMs for constraint mining could be a concern for very large API specifications.  The paper does not extensively discuss scalability considerations.
    * **Type of Constraints:** The paper focuses on certain types of constraints and there is room for extending the kinds of constraints that the system could mine and test.
* **Influence on the Field:**  This paper can influence the field of API testing by promoting the use of combined static/dynamic analysis techniques.  It also demonstrates the potential of LLMs for automating constraint mining, especially when combined with techniques to mitigate their limitations.  Future research can build upon this work by exploring different LLM architectures, constraint types, and verification strategies.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in API testing. The combination of static and dynamic analysis, coupled with LLM-specific enhancements, is a valuable contribution.  The empirical evaluation is thorough and demonstrates the practical relevance of the work. While the approach is dependent on the quality of API specifications and LLMs do have some known weaknesses, the authors address this limitation effectively through the design of RBCTEST. The work has a clear potential for impact on the practice of API testing. The score reflects the significant novelty and the careful evaluation of the work, while also accounting for the limitations around dependence on specification and LLM behavior.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### AI-Enhanced Business Process Automation: A Case Study in the Insurance Domain Using Object-Centric Process Mining
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17295v1)
- **Authors**: Shahrzad Khayatbashi, Viktor Sjölind, Anders Granåker, Amin Jalali
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Artificial Intelligence (AI), particularly Large Language Models (LLMs), have enhanced organizations' ability to reengineer business processes by automating knowledge-intensive tasks. This automation drives digital transformation, often through gradual transitions that improve process efficiency and effectiveness. To fully assess the impact of such automation, a data-driven analysis approach is needed - one that examines how traditional and AI-enhanced process variants coexist during this transition. Object-Centric Process Mining (OCPM) has emerged as a valuable method that enables such analysis, yet real-world case studies are still needed to demonstrate its applicability. This paper presents a case study from the insurance sector, where an LLM was deployed in production to automate the identification of claim parts, a task previously performed manually and identified as a bottleneck for scalability. To evaluate this transformation, we apply OCPM to assess the impact of AI-driven automation on process scalability. Our findings indicate that while LLMs significantly enhance operational capacity, they also introduce new process dynamics that require further refinement. This study also demonstrates the practical application of OCPM in a real-world setting, highlighting its advantages and limitations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of AI, specifically Large Language Models (LLMs), on business process automation in the insurance domain, focusing on the task of claim part identification. It uses Object-Centric Process Mining (OCPM) to analyze the transition from a manual (as-is) process to an AI-enhanced (to-be) workflow. The study analyzes data from a real-world implementation where an LLM was deployed to automate the identification of claim parts.  The results show that while the LLM significantly increases scalability in identification, it also introduces new bottlenecks and requires adjustments in downstream processes. The paper also discusses the challenges in communicating OCPM findings to business stakeholders.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Strengths:*

*   **Real-world Case Study:** The paper is grounded in a practical application of AI in a specific business context (insurance claims). This provides valuable insights beyond theoretical discussions.
*   **OCPM Application:** The use of OCPM to analyze the impact of AI-driven process changes is a key strength. It allows for a comprehensive, data-driven assessment that goes beyond simply measuring efficiency gains. It highlights the complex interplay between different process perspectives.
*   **Identified Bottleneck Shift:** A significant finding is that AI implementation, while improving one aspect of the process, can shift bottlenecks to other areas. This demonstrates the importance of holistic process reengineering rather than focusing solely on individual task automation.
*   **Addresses a Gap in Literature:** The paper directly addresses the lack of real-world case studies demonstrating the application of OCPM in analyzing process transformations.
*   **Evaluation Framework:** The structured approach using BPR, CRISP-DM, and OCPM² provides a systematic evaluation framework that can be adapted for other studies.
*   **Focus on Practical Challenges:** The paper acknowledges the challenges in communication and visualization when using complex process mining techniques with business stakeholders.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Generalizability:** The study focuses on a specific line of business within a single insurance company. While valuable, the findings may not be directly generalizable to other industries or even other departments within the same company.
*   **Lack of a Control Group:** While comparing 'as-is' to 'to-be', there isn't a clear control group (e.g., a similar process *without* LLM intervention, running concurrently). This limits the ability to definitively attribute all changes solely to the AI implementation.
*   **Confidentiality Limitations:** The inability to disclose specific business terminology or the exact LLM prompts hinders the replication of the study.
*   **OCPM Communication Challenges:**  While admitting the communication challenges of the OCPM analysis, the paper doesn't present novel techniques to mitigate these barriers. It only reiterates that flattened DFGs were found to be more useful.
*   **Limited Depth of LLM Analysis:** While the paper evaluates the LLM's recall and precision, it doesn't delve deeply into the LLM's internal workings or the rationale behind specific prompt engineering choices.  More analysis of why certain claim parts were missed could be useful.
*   **The study doesn't use sophisticated process mining techniques to provide more detailed analysis beyond the baseline OCPM. For example, using conformance checking would provide the true deviation from the process.
*   **Not extremely novel in Process Mining:** The application of OCPM is not new to process mining. The focus in AI and its effect is not novel.

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper's novelty lies in its *combined* application of OCPM to *analyze the impact of AI-driven automation in a real-world setting*. While individual elements (AI in process automation, OCPM) are not novel, the integration is, providing practical insights into the complexities of process reengineering with AI. Its significance is in providing an empirical understanding of how AI can affect different process elements, and the necessity of addressing new constraints. This is valuable for organizations considering AI implementation.

**Overall Score:**

Given the strengths (real-world application, OCPM, identified bottleneck shift) and weaknesses (limited generalizability, confidentiality, lack of novel techniques to mitigate the communication barriers of OCPM), I assign the paper a score of:

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper is a well-executed case study that offers valuable practical insights into the use of AI for business process automation. It provides a concrete example of how OCPM can be used to analyze the complex impacts of AI on process dynamics. The findings regarding bottleneck shifting and the importance of holistic process design are particularly significant. However, the limited generalizability and lack of novel visualization improvements to OCPM, limit its overall impact on the field. Additionally, the study itself is not a particularly novel application in PM. However, a strong case study that builds on a PM framework.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### CoheMark: A Novel Sentence-Level Watermark for Enhanced Text Quality
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17309v1)
- **Authors**: Junyan Zhang, Shuliang Liu, Aiwei Liu, Yubo Gao, Jungang Li, Xiaojie Gu, Xuming Hu
- **Abstract**: Watermarking technology is a method used to trace the usage of content generated by large language models. Sentence-level watermarking aids in preserving the semantic integrity within individual sentences while maintaining greater robustness. However, many existing sentence-level watermarking techniques depend on arbitrary segmentation or generation processes to embed watermarks, which can limit the availability of appropriate sentences. This limitation, in turn, compromises the quality of the generated response. To address the challenge of balancing high text quality with robust watermark detection, we propose CoheMark, an advanced sentence-level watermarking technique that exploits the cohesive relationships between sentences for better logical fluency. The core methodology of CoheMark involves selecting sentences through trained fuzzy c-means clustering and applying specific next sentence selection criteria. Experimental evaluations demonstrate that CoheMark achieves strong watermark strength while exerting minimal impact on text quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoheMark, a novel sentence-level watermarking technique designed to improve the quality of watermarked text generated by large language models (LLMs).  CoheMark focuses on preserving the cohesive relationships between sentences to maintain logical fluency. It achieves this through a process involving fuzzy c-means clustering of sentences to divide the semantic space, followed by specific criteria for selecting the next sentence based on the semantics of the preceding sentence. The method consists of three modules: Embedder, FuzzyClusterer, and CoheSampler. Experimental results show that CoheMark maintains strong watermark detection capabilities while minimizing the impact on text quality, compared to existing methods. The paper evaluates the approach on two datasets (OpenGen and LFQA) and two LLMs (OPT-2.7B and Llama-3-8B), comparing against several baselines. It also includes an evaluation of robustness against paraphrasing attacks and a qualitative analysis based on LLM-based (GPT-40) pairwise preference evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The core idea of using sentence cohesion for watermarking is a valuable direction. While previous sentence-level watermarking techniques exist, CoheMark explicitly exploits the inter-sentence dependencies and logical flow. The use of fuzzy c-means clustering for semantic space division in this context is a reasonable approach.  However, the individual components (fuzzy clustering, sentence embeddings) are not novel *per se*; the novelty lies in their integration within the watermarking framework. The selection criteria (NSSCv1 and NSSCv2) and switching rule seem like somewhat ad-hoc choices that could benefit from a more principled justification or optimization.
* **Significance:** The problem of balancing watermarking effectiveness and text quality is crucial for the adoption of LLMs. CoheMark's ability to maintain a high detection rate while minimizing quality degradation is a significant contribution. The comparison with several baselines provides strong evidence for its effectiveness. The inclusion of LLM-based evaluations (using GPT-40) to assess text quality moves beyond traditional metrics (perplexity, BertScore), which is a strength. This represents a more comprehensive assessment of the impact on generated text.
* **Strengths:**
    * The paper addresses an important problem in LLM watermarking.
    * The proposed CoheMark technique incorporates a clear and intuitive idea – sentence cohesion.
    * The experimental results provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of CoheMark.
    * The evaluation of text quality using LLM-based metrics is a positive aspect.
    * Demonstrates robustness against paraphrasing attacks.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The specific sentence selection criteria (NSSCv1, NSSCv2, switching rule) seem somewhat ad-hoc and require further justification. There could be a more systematic optimization strategy for these rules.
    * The individual components of the method are not particularly novel.
    * The generation speeds, although better than SemStamp, are still significantly slower than token-level watermarks. This could limit its applicability in some settings.
    * Some claims, like CoheMark generating "more engaging content," lack objective measurement and are mostly qualitative.
    * There are limitations to the model. In some cases it cannot generate watermark text.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future watermarking techniques for LLMs, emphasizing the importance of sentence cohesion. The use of LLM-based evaluations can become a standard practice in this area.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7

**Rationale:**

CoheMark represents a meaningful step forward in sentence-level watermarking. It provides a compelling approach to balancing watermarking effectiveness and text quality by explicitly considering sentence cohesion. While the individual components may not be groundbreaking and some design choices could be more rigorously justified, the overall integration is novel and the results are impressive. The comprehensive evaluation, including LLM-based metrics, strengthens the validity of the findings.  The significant lag in generation speed compared to token-level methods and rare failures to generate watermarked text does limit its applicability, preventing a higher score. However, the overall contribution to the field is significant enough to warrant a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FLUKE: A Linguistically-Driven and Task-Agnostic Framework for Robustness Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17311v1)
- **Authors**: Yulia Otmakhova, Hung Thinh Truong, Rahmad Mahendra, Zenan Zhai, Rongxin Zhu, Daniel Beck, Jey Han Lau
- **Abstract**: We present FLUKE (Framework for LingUistically-driven and tasK-agnostic robustness Evaluation), a task-agnostic framework for assessing model robustness through systematic minimal variations of test data. FLUKE introduces controlled variations across linguistic levels - from orthography to dialect and style varieties - and leverages large language models (LLMs) with human validation to generate modifications. We demonstrate FLUKE's utility by evaluating both fine-tuned models and LLMs across four diverse NLP tasks, and reveal that (1) the impact of linguistic variations is highly task-dependent, with some tests being critical for certain tasks but irrelevant for others; (2) while LLMs have better overall robustness compared to fine-tuned models, they still exhibit significant brittleness to certain linguistic variations; (3) all models show substantial vulnerability to negation modifications across most tasks. These findings highlight the importance of systematic robustness testing for understanding model behaviors.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FLUKE, a framework for evaluating the robustness of NLP models. FLUKE systematically introduces minimal linguistic variations to existing test data across multiple linguistic levels (orthography, morphology, syntax, semantics, discourse, style, dialect). These variations are generated using LLMs and validated through human annotation. The authors demonstrate FLUKE's utility by evaluating fine-tuned PLMs and LLMs on four diverse NLP tasks, revealing task-specific vulnerabilities and limitations in the robustness of even large language models.  The authors find that (1) robustness depends heavily on the task and types of modifications, (2) while LLMs are more robust than PLMs, they still exhibit brittleness, and (3) all models struggle with negation. The authors release their code and data to facilitate further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Systematic Approach:** The paper's strength lies in its systematic approach to robustness evaluation. FLUKE provides a structured methodology that goes beyond ad-hoc or adversarial testing, offering a more comprehensive view of model vulnerabilities.
*   **Linguistic Breadth:**  FLUKE covers a wide range of linguistic phenomena, from surface-level orthographic variations to more complex semantic and discourse-level modifications. This is a more realistic assessment of NLP model capabilities.
*   **Task Agnostic:** The framework is designed to be task-agnostic, making it applicable to a wide range of NLP tasks and model architectures. This increases its generalizability and value.
*   **Human Validation:** Incorporating human validation in the modification process is crucial. It ensures that the generated variations are linguistically valid and target the intended properties, increasing the reliability of the evaluation.
*   **Actionable Insights:** The paper provides specific and actionable insights into model vulnerabilities. For example, the finding that all models are significantly vulnerable to negation provides a clear direction for future research.
*   **Reproducibility:** The release of code, data, and prompts allows for easy reproduction and extension of the work.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **LLM Reliance:** While leveraging LLMs for variation generation is efficient, it also introduces potential biases. The modifications may reflect the LLM's own limitations or biases, potentially underestimating or skewing model vulnerabilities.
*   **Human Validation Bottleneck:**  Even with automated generation, human validation remains a bottleneck. Scaling the framework to larger datasets or more linguistic phenomena requires significant human effort.
*   **Limited Task Scope:** While the paper explores four diverse NLP tasks, it would be valuable to evaluate FLUKE on an even wider range of tasks, including generative tasks or tasks with different input modalities (e.g., image captioning).
*   **Metric Sensitivity:** The weighted delta metric, while aiming to highlight the relative change in model performance, might obscure absolute performance differences, especially when baseline performance is already low.

**Novelty and Significance:**

FLUKE presents a significant advance over existing approaches to robustness evaluation. While previous works have focused on specific types of variations or adversarial attacks, FLUKE offers a more comprehensive and systematic framework. The task-agnostic design and the integration of human validation are key contributions.  The findings regarding the task-specific nature of model vulnerabilities and the pervasive challenge of negation are valuable insights that can guide future research in NLP model development.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence how NLP models are evaluated and developed. FLUKE provides a valuable tool for model developers to identify and address vulnerabilities, leading to more robust and reliable NLP systems. The framework can also serve as a benchmark for comparing the robustness of different models.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **8** to this paper. It presents a solid, well-executed framework with significant strengths in its systematic approach, linguistic breadth, task-agnostic design, and actionable insights. The paper demonstrates a clear understanding of the existing literature and addresses a critical challenge in NLP. The weaknesses related to LLM reliance and the human validation bottleneck are valid concerns but do not significantly detract from the overall contribution. The potential influence of FLUKE on robustness evaluation and model development justifies a high score. The framework allows systematic diagnosis of vulnerabilities which is extremely valuable.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### DIMT25@ICDAR2025: HW-TSC's End-to-End Document Image Machine Translation System Leveraging Large Vision-Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17315v1)
- **Authors**: Zhanglin Wu, Tengfei Song, Ning Xie, Weidong Zhang, Pengfei Li, Shuang Wu, Chong Li, Junhao Zhu, Hao Yang
- **Abstract**: This paper presents the technical solution proposed by Huawei Translation Service Center (HW-TSC) for the "End-to-End Document Image Machine Translation for Complex Layouts" competition at the 19th International Conference on Document Analysis and Recognition (DIMT25@ICDAR2025). Leveraging state-of-the-art open-source large vision-language model (LVLM), we introduce a training framework that combines multi-task learning with perceptual chain-of-thought to develop a comprehensive end-to-end document translation system. During the inference phase, we apply minimum Bayesian decoding and post-processing strategies to further enhance the system's translation capabilities. Our solution uniquely addresses both OCR-based and OCR-free document image translation tasks within a unified framework. This paper systematically details the training methods, inference strategies, LVLM base models, training data, experimental setups, and results, demonstrating an effective approach to document image machine translation.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper describes the HW-TSC team's approach to the "End-to-End Document Image Machine Translation for Complex Layouts" competition at ICDAR 2025. The core of their solution involves fine-tuning large vision-language models (LVLMs) using a combination of multi-task learning (MTL) and perceptual chain-of-thought (PCOT) training. This aims to enable the model to simultaneously understand visual layouts and linguistic content in documents. During inference, they use minimum Bayesian decoding (MBR) and post-processing techniques to enhance translation quality. The paper emphasizes a unified framework for both OCR-based and OCR-free document image translation tasks. They detail their training methodology, inference strategies, base LVLMs, training data, and experimental results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Leveraging LVLMs:** Using and fine-tuning state-of-the-art LVLMs is a logical and potentially effective approach to this complex task. Document image translation requires both visual understanding and language generation, which LVLMs are designed for.
    *   **MTL and PCOT:** The combination of multi-task learning (MTL) and perceptual chain-of-thought (PCOT) training shows promise. MTL can help the model generalize better across related tasks (e.g., OCR, layout understanding, translation), and PCOT encourages a more structured and explainable reasoning process, which is beneficial for complex document processing.
    *   **Unified Framework:** The emphasis on a unified framework for both OCR-based and OCR-free scenarios is a good design choice. This makes the system more adaptable to real-world situations where OCR quality may vary or be unavailable.
    *   **MBR Decoding and Post-Processing:** Applying MBR decoding and post-processing techniques suggests a practical approach to improving output quality. MBR helps to select the best hypothesis from a set of candidates, while post-processing can address specific errors or inconsistencies in the translation.
    *   **Clear and Systematic Presentation:** The paper strives for reproducibility by detailing their approach. The authors specifically mention their effort to be transparent about their training data and configurations.
    *   **Demonstrated improvements**: The result section shows significant improvements due to the training and inference approaches.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The techniques themselves (LVLMs, MTL, PCOT, MBR) are not entirely novel. The novelty likely lies in *how* they are combined and applied to the specific problem of document image translation, and how these techniques are adapted within the context of the DIMT25 competition, but the paper needs to explicitly articulate that more forcefully. It's not enough to *use* existing techniques; the contribution is in the *adaptation, implementation, and evaluation* of those techniques.
    *   **Limited Depth of Analysis:** While the paper presents results, it lacks a deep analysis of *why* certain techniques work or fail. For instance, it would be valuable to understand the types of errors that MBR and post-processing are correcting and the impact of different MTL training regimes. Error analysis and ablation studies would strengthen the paper significantly.
    *   **Competition-Specific Focus:** The paper is heavily geared toward the DIMT25 competition. While this is understandable, it somewhat limits the generalizability of the findings. It would be more impactful if the paper discussed the broader applicability of their approach to other document image translation tasks or datasets.
    *   **Dependency on Open Source Components:** While using open-source models and frameworks is sensible, the paper could benefit from discussing the limitations or challenges encountered with these components and any modifications or adaptations made to them.

*   **Significance:**

    *   If the method reported results in the top rankings of the DIMT25 competition it would raise the significance of this work.
    *   The significance lies in the practical application of these methods within a complex task like document translation, and demonstrating how these techniques can be efficiently applied for such tasks. The emphasis on end-to-end processing and a unified framework are useful contributions. However, the ultimate significance depends on the impact it has on the broader research community and the extent to which others build upon their work.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **6**.

*Rationale:*

The paper presents a solid engineering approach to a challenging problem. It leverages established techniques (LVLMs, MTL, PCOT, MBR) effectively within the context of a document image translation competition. The emphasis on a unified framework and practical implementation is valuable.

However, the paper's novelty is somewhat limited. It doesn't introduce fundamentally new techniques, and the analysis of results could be more in-depth. The competition-specific focus also slightly restricts its broader impact. I am assigning the score assuming the results reported were not among the top in the competition, in which case the score would be lowered. While the approach might be considered novel to the context of this specific competition, the individual components are known to the community. A stronger articulation of the *specific innovations* and *detailed* analysis of the error reductions would bolster the score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Exploring Context-aware and LLM-driven Locomotion for Immersive Virtual Reality
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17331v1)
- **Authors**: Süleyman Özdel, Kadir Burak Buldu, Enkelejda Kasneci, Efe Bozkir
- **Abstract**: Locomotion plays a crucial role in shaping the user experience within virtual reality environments. In particular, hands-free locomotion offers a valuable alternative by supporting accessibility and freeing users from reliance on handheld controllers. To this end, traditional speech-based methods often depend on rigid command sets, limiting the naturalness and flexibility of interaction. In this study, we propose a novel locomotion technique powered by large language models (LLMs), which allows users to navigate virtual environments using natural language with contextual awareness. We evaluate three locomotion methods: controller-based teleportation, voice-based steering, and our language model-driven approach. Our evaluation measures include eye-tracking data analysis, including explainable machine learning through SHAP analysis as well as standardized questionnaires for usability, presence, cybersickness, and cognitive load to examine user attention and engagement. Our findings indicate that the LLM-driven locomotion possesses comparable usability, presence, and cybersickness scores to established methods like teleportation, demonstrating its novel potential as a comfortable, natural language-based, hands-free alternative. In addition, it enhances user attention within the virtual environment, suggesting greater engagement. Complementary to these findings, SHAP analysis revealed that fixation, saccade, and pupil-related features vary across techniques, indicating distinct patterns of visual attention and cognitive processing. Overall, we state that our method can facilitate hands-free locomotion in virtual spaces, especially in supporting accessibility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores a novel locomotion technique for immersive virtual reality (VR) that leverages large language models (LLMs) to enable natural language-based, hands-free navigation.  Instead of relying on pre-defined voice commands, the system allows users to navigate VR environments by providing free-form spoken instructions, with the LLM interpreting intent based on contextual information from the virtual scene. The study compares this LLM-driven approach with controller-based teleportation and voice-based steering (using fixed commands) across several metrics, including task completion time, eye-tracking data (fixations, saccades, pupil diameter), usability (SUS), presence (IPQ), cybersickness (CSQ-VR), and cognitive load (NASA-TLX). The results indicate that the LLM-driven locomotion offers comparable usability, presence, and cybersickness levels to established methods like teleportation, while also enhancing user attention. The study also uses SHAP analysis to identify key eye-tracking features that differentiate the three locomotion techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs for natural language, context-aware VR locomotion is novel and addresses a significant limitation of traditional voice-based VR navigation, which typically relies on rigid command sets. Integrating LLMs with real-time scene understanding to interpret user intent and map it to valid navigable locations demonstrates a meaningful step forward. The SHAP analysis of eye-tracking data to reveal nuanced differences in attentional patterns across locomotion techniques further enhances the paper's novelty.

*   **Significance:** The work has potential significance for the VR field, particularly concerning accessibility and improved user experience. By enabling hands-free navigation through natural language, the method could make VR more accessible to users with motor impairments or in situations where hands are otherwise occupied. The findings suggesting enhanced user attention with the LLM-driven approach point to greater engagement and potential for improved learning or task performance in VR environments. The study also contributes to the broader understanding of how different locomotion techniques affect cognitive processing and visual attention.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem and solution:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing VR locomotion techniques and proposes a well-motivated solution based on LLMs.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:**  The user study employs a wide range of metrics, including subjective questionnaires and objective eye-tracking data, providing a thorough assessment of the proposed technique.
    *   **Use of SHAP analysis:** Applying explainable AI techniques (SHAP) adds depth to the analysis by revealing feature importance and providing insights into how different eye-tracking features contribute to classifying the locomotion techniques.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and structured, with clear explanations of the methodology, results, and discussion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Latency:** The paper mentions the latency of the LLM as a system overhead and notes the end-to-end processing time is kept under 1.5s. This may be problematic for some users. 
    *   **Dependence on specific LLM:** The study relies on a specific LLM (ChatGPT-4o), which may limit the generalizability of the findings. The performance and behavior of the LLM-driven locomotion could vary depending on the specific LLM used.
    *   **Limited environment complexity:** The virtual environment used in the study, while realistic, had limited complexity.  More complex environments with a larger number of objects and potential ambiguities in natural language commands might expose limitations in the system's ability to accurately interpret user intent.
    *   **Small sample size:** A sample size of 20 users per locomotion condition may limit the statistical power of the analysis.

*   **Impact:** The paper could influence future research in VR locomotion by promoting the use of LLMs and context-aware approaches. The eye-tracking analysis and SHAP results could guide the design of adaptive VR systems that dynamically adjust locomotion techniques based on user attention and cognitive state.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and potentially impactful approach, several limitations prevent it from achieving a higher score. The reliance on a specific, proprietary LLM, limited environment complexity, and sample size constraints temper the broader applicability and generalizability of the findings. The latency of LLM could be a problem with users.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Bridging Cognition and Emotion: Empathy-Driven Multimodal Misinformation Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17332v1)
- **Authors**: Zihan Wang, Lu Yuan, Zhengxuan Zhang, Qing Zhao
- **Abstract**: In the digital era, social media has become a major conduit for information dissemination, yet it also facilitates the rapid spread of misinformation. Traditional misinformation detection methods primarily focus on surface-level features, overlooking the crucial roles of human empathy in the propagation process. To address this gap, we propose the Dual-Aspect Empathy Framework (DAE), which integrates cognitive and emotional empathy to analyze misinformation from both the creator and reader perspectives. By examining creators' cognitive strategies and emotional appeals, as well as simulating readers' cognitive judgments and emotional responses using Large Language Models (LLMs), DAE offers a more comprehensive and human-centric approach to misinformation detection. Moreover, we further introduce an empathy-aware filtering mechanism to enhance response authenticity and diversity. Experimental results on benchmark datasets demonstrate that DAE outperforms existing methods, providing a novel paradigm for multimodal misinformation detection.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel "Dual-Aspect Empathy Framework" (DAE) for multimodal misinformation detection.  It addresses the limitation of existing methods that primarily focus on surface-level features and neglect the role of human empathy in the spread of misinformation. DAE integrates both cognitive and emotional empathy from the perspectives of content creators and readers. It analyzes creators' strategies and emotional appeals while simulating readers' cognitive judgements and emotional responses using LLMs.  An empathy-aware filtering mechanism is introduced to ensure authenticity and diversity of simulated responses. Experiments on benchmark datasets demonstrate the superiority of DAE over existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its explicit integration of empathy (cognitive and emotional) into a misinformation detection framework. Existing methods often consider emotions in the content, but DAE's dual-aspect approach, explicitly modeling the *creator's* intent and *reader's* interpretation through empathy, is a significant contribution. The LLM-based comment generation and empathy-aware filtering are also innovative additions.

*   **Significance:** Misinformation is a major problem, and enhancing detection methods is crucial. The incorporation of empathy adds a layer of understanding that surface-level analysis often misses. The experimental results show a consistent improvement over existing approaches on two well-known datasets, suggesting that the DAE framework has the potential to improve the accuracy of misinformation detection. By considering the underlying psychological aspects involved in creating and consuming misinformation, the approach is significant as it can help understand the deeper mechanisms of spread.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework provides a clear and understandable approach to incorporating empathy into misinformation detection.
    *   The use of LLMs for comment generation is clever and addresses the problem of obtaining diverse perspectives.
    *   The experimental results consistently show improvement over strong baselines.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of the framework.
    *   The case studies offer qualitative examples that show the utility of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on LLMs for comment generation introduces a potential bias. The generated comments might not perfectly reflect real-world user behavior and empathy. The effectiveness is heavily dependent on the prompting strategy and capabilities of the LLM used (GPT-4 in this case). Future LLMs could perform differently. The filtering mechanism is also crucial but its effectiveness isn't independently tested as part of the ablation study.
    *   While two benchmark datasets are used, the generalizability to other types of misinformation (e.g., visual, video) needs further investigation. Specifically, is it the case that most textual information can be used in some way as an auxiliary to the DAE?
    *   The details of the "empathy-aware filtering mechanism" could be more elaborate. The exact criteria and process by which comments are filtered based on empathy should be more transparent.
    *   The connection of demographic information to the DAE framework could be more explicit. The connection between comment diversity and reader profiles is also quite implicit.

*   **Impact:** The paper can influence future research on misinformation detection, encouraging researchers to consider psychological factors and human-centric approaches. It can also inspire the development of more sophisticated LLM-based methods for generating realistic and diverse user responses.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel and promising approach to misinformation detection by integrating empathy into the process.  It addresses an important limitation of existing methods and demonstrates improved performance on benchmark datasets.  While the reliance on LLMs introduces some potential biases and the framework's generalizability requires further study, the core idea of incorporating empathy is significant. A detailed description of the experimental framework makes it easy to reproduce. The improvement achieved on benchmark datasets is also statistically significant.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fine-Grained Fusion: The Missing Piece in Area-Efficient State Space Model Acceleration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17333v1)
- **Authors**: Robin Geens, Arne Symons, Marian Verhelst
- **Abstract**: State Space Models (SSMs) offer a promising alternative to transformers for long-sequence processing. However, their efficiency remains hindered by memory-bound operations, particularly in the prefill stage. While MARCA, a recent first effort to accelerate SSMs through a dedicated hardware accelerator, achieves great speedup over high-end GPUs, an analysis into the broader accelerator design space is lacking. This work systematically analyzes SSM acceleration opportunities both from the scheduling perspective through fine-grained operator fusion and the hardware perspective through design space exploration, using an extended version of the Stream modeling framework. Our results demonstrate that the improved data locality stemming from our optimized fusion and scheduling strategy enables a speedup of up to 4.8x over unfused execution, while our adaptive memory-aware fusion approach reduces on-chip memory requirements by an order of magnitude without sacrificing performance. We further explore accelerator design trade-offs, showing that a fusion-aware hardware architecture can achieve 1.78x higher performance than the state-of-the-art MARCA accelerator, within the same area budget. These results establish operator fusion as a key enabler for next-generation SSM accelerators.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates hardware acceleration strategies for State Space Models (SSMs), focusing on improving memory access efficiency during the prefill stage, a bottleneck for SSM performance.  It extends the Stream modeling framework to analyze and optimize SSM execution, specifically exploring operator fusion techniques to enhance data locality. The paper demonstrates that optimized operator fusion and scheduling strategies can significantly improve performance and reduce on-chip memory requirements. Furthermore, it explores the hardware accelerator design space, showing that a fusion-aware architecture can outperform existing solutions (MARCA) within the same area budget. The central argument is that fine-grained operator fusion is crucial for realizing the area-efficient potential of SSMs on dedicated hardware.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic analysis of SSM acceleration opportunities, particularly the application of operator fusion strategies for improved data locality. While operator fusion is well-established in the context of transformer acceleration, its comprehensive exploration within the SSM domain, combined with hardware design space exploration, is a valuable contribution.  Extending the Stream modeling framework with specific support for SSMs (including the state update mechanism, novel dependency tracking, and multi-cycle operator modeling) is also a notable practical contribution that enables further research.  The exploration of a memory-aware fusion scheme is another specific novel aspect.

* **Significance:** The findings are significant because SSMs offer a promising alternative to transformers for long-sequence processing but need optimization for hardware efficiency to realize their potential. Demonstrating a 4.8x speedup through fusion and a significant reduction in memory requirements (while maintaining performance) is impactful. The paper's hardware design space exploration, which reveals the sub-optimality of existing designs like MARCA and suggests alternative architectural configurations, provides concrete guidance for future accelerator development. By addressing the memory bottleneck that hampers SSM performance, this research accelerates their practical adoption.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Systematic Approach:** The paper takes a rigorous and systematic approach, starting with a high-level analysis and drilling down into detailed modeling and optimization.
    * **Quantitative Results:** The paper provides thorough quantitative results, comparing various fusion strategies and hardware configurations, and provides a clear path to performance improvements. The roofline analysis is effective.
    * **Practical Contributions:** The extension of the Stream framework is a valuable tool for the community.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents the methodology, results, and conclusions. The figures and tables are informative.
    * **Direct Comparison:** The comparison to MARCA makes the implications of the work more tangible and convincing.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Sequential State Update:** The choice of a sequential state update implementation, while justified, limits the exploration of potentially more parallel approaches. The paper acknowledges this trade-off. While this is justified in the context of the target architecture, it omits a broader perspective.
    * **Limited Hardware Design Space:** While the hardware design space exploration is valuable, it is still parameterized by the MARCA accelerator. A deeper exploration of more radically different architectures could be beneficial, but is limited by simulation constraints.
    * **Simplified Cost Model:** The memory area/performance cost model is a simplification. A more detailed hardware-level analysis (e.g., using synthesis results) would strengthen the conclusions, but would come with a high simulation cost.
    * **Lack of Validation:** The predicted performance gains are based on simulation. Implementation and hardware measurements are missing, which would lend more credibility to the work.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the design of future SSM accelerators and stimulate further research in this area. The optimized fusion strategies and hardware design insights provide a solid foundation for developing more efficient and scalable SSM implementations. The extension of the Stream framework also provides a valuable tool for other researchers in the field.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper doesn't present a completely revolutionary concept, it makes a significant contribution to the emerging field of SSM acceleration. The systematic analysis, quantitative results, practical tools, and tangible architectural insights justify a strong score. The limitations mentioned above, primarily the simulation-based nature and parameterization based on the MARCA architecture, prevent it from achieving a higher score. It's a solid advancement built upon existing methods (operator fusion) but targeted to a new and promising architecture.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.AR
- **Score**: 7/10

### TimeChat-Online: 80% Visual Tokens are Naturally Redundant in Streaming Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17343v1)
- **Authors**: Linli Yao, Yicheng Li, Yuancheng Wei, Lei Li, Shuhuai Ren, Yuanxin Liu, Kun Ouyang, Lean Wang, Shicheng Li, Sida Li, Lingpeng Kong, Qi Liu, Yuanxing Zhang, Xu Sun
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of online video platforms, particularly live streaming services, has created an urgent need for real-time video understanding systems. These systems must process continuous video streams and respond to user queries instantaneously, presenting unique challenges for current Video Large Language Models (VideoLLMs). While existing VideoLLMs excel at processing complete videos, they face significant limitations in streaming scenarios due to their inability to handle dense, redundant frames efficiently. We introduce TimeChat-Online, a novel online VideoLLM that revolutionizes real-time video interaction. At its core lies our innovative Differential Token Drop (DTD) module, which addresses the fundamental challenge of visual redundancy in streaming videos. Drawing inspiration from human visual perception's Change Blindness phenomenon, DTD preserves meaningful temporal changes while filtering out static, redundant content between frames. Remarkably, our experiments demonstrate that DTD achieves an 82.8% reduction in video tokens while maintaining 98% performance on StreamingBench, revealing that over 80% of visual content in streaming videos is naturally redundant without requiring language guidance. To enable seamless real-time interaction, we present TimeChat-Online-139K, a comprehensive streaming video dataset featuring diverse interaction patterns including backward-tracing, current-perception, and future-responding scenarios. TimeChat-Online's unique Proactive Response capability, naturally achieved through continuous monitoring of video scene transitions via DTD, sets it apart from conventional approaches. Our extensive evaluation demonstrates TimeChat-Online's superior performance on streaming benchmarks (StreamingBench and OvOBench) and maintaining competitive results on long-form video tasks such as Video-MME and MLVU.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TimeChat-Online, a novel online Video Large Language Model (VideoLLM) designed for efficient real-time interaction with streaming video. The core innovation is the Differential Token Drop (DTD) module, which selectively preserves significant temporal changes across video streams while filtering out redundant visual information. DTD is inspired by the human "change blindness" phenomenon. The paper demonstrates that DTD significantly reduces video token count (by 82.8%) while maintaining high accuracy (98% of original) on a streaming benchmark. The authors also contribute TimeChat-Online-139K, a new streaming video dataset featuring diverse interaction patterns. Experiments show TimeChat-Online achieves state-of-the-art performance on streaming benchmarks and competitive results on long-form video tasks. Integrating DTD with Qwen2.5VL-7B improves accuracy on a challenging VideoMME subset while reducing video tokens.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a critical challenge in the VideoLLM space – the inefficiency of processing dense, redundant frames in streaming video. This problem is becoming increasingly important with the rise of live streaming and real-time video applications.
    *   **Novelty of DTD:** The Differential Token Drop (DTD) module represents a novel approach to visual redundancy reduction. It's an elegant, vision-based method that doesn't rely on language guidance, making it computationally efficient for streaming scenarios. The bio-inspired approach from change blindness provides a solid conceptual foundation.
    *   **Significant Performance Gains:** The experimental results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of DTD. The substantial reduction in video tokens (over 80%) with minimal impact on accuracy is a significant achievement.  The speedup in inference latency is also highly valuable.
    *   **New Dataset Contribution:** The TimeChat-Online-139K dataset fills a gap in the availability of data specifically designed for streaming VideoQA, addressing different interaction patterns and incorporating proactive responding.
    *   **Strong Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a comprehensive evaluation across various benchmarks, comparing against both offline and online VideoLLMs and analyzing the performance impact of different design choices. The ablation studies and breakdown analyses provide valuable insights.
    * **Seamless LLM integration:**  The ability to integrate DTD seamlessly into established VideoLLMs, like Qwen2.5-VL,  demonstrates the DTD's practical utility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Nature of the Dataset:** While TimeChat-Online-139K is a valuable contribution, its synthetic generation raises concerns about its realism and generalizability to real-world streaming video interaction scenarios.  GPT-4o annotations might introduce biases.

    *   **Limited Novelty in the End-to-End System:** The paper focuses primarily on DTD. While effective, the rest of the TimeChat-Online architecture appears to be built upon existing components (Qwen2.5-VL). The real significance would be if DTD could unlock completely new architectures for streaming.

    *   **Scope for Deeper Analysis:**  While the paper provides comprehensive results, further investigation into the types of videos and queries where DTD is most effective could offer valuable insights. A more detailed analysis of the failure cases and limitations of DTD would strengthen the work. The decision-making that led to values for *τ* is not that well described.

    *   **Lack of Direct Comparison:** While the authors compare against current state-of-the-art performance, showing a direct comparison to current algorithms would further strengthen their claims.

    * **Position reservation**: While a good idea, what evidence does the paper have that this is critical?

*   **Significance and Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to significantly impact the development of more efficient and responsive VideoLLMs for streaming applications.
    *   The DTD module could be adopted as a standard technique for visual redundancy reduction in streaming video processing.
    *   The TimeChat-Online-139K dataset could serve as a valuable resource for training and evaluating future streaming VideoLLMs.
    *   The work could inspire further research into bio-inspired approaches for efficient video understanding.

**Justification for the Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **8/10** to this paper.

*Rationale:* The paper addresses a significant problem with a novel and effective solution (DTD). The experimental results are compelling, and the dataset contribution is valuable. However, the reliance on a synthetic dataset and the incremental nature of the end-to-end system (beyond DTD itself) limit its overall novelty and impact. The results would be significantly strengthened by empirical analyses to address some of the weaknesses described above.
